## Document 034: 2026年4～6月期のGDP（2次速報）結果

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年4～6月期のGDP（2次速報）結果.pdf
Report source: MURC
Date: 2026-09-08
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1 / 2

2026 年 9 月 8 日

調査レポート

2026 年 4～6 月期の GDP（2 次速報）結果

～1 次速報から小幅上方修正されたが、景気の評価に変更なし～

調査部 主任研究員 藤田隼平

2026 年 9 月 8 日に内閣府が公表した 4～6 月期の実質 GDP（2 次速報値）は、前期比＋0.4%（年率換算   
＋1.4%）と、8 月に公表された 1 次速報値の同＋0.3%（同換算＋1.1%）から小幅ながら上方修正された。緩やか
な景気回復が続いているとの評価に変わりはなく、米国とイランの対立が逆風となったが、政府の物価高対策や
代替調達の進展などが下支えとなったこともあり、当初懸念されたような物価高や供給制約が景気全体を下押し
する事態は回避された。 
実質 GDP の需要項目別に 1 次速報からの 主な修正状況をみると、 まず個人消費が前期比－0.0% から   
＋0.0%に小幅に上方修正された。他方、 住宅投資は前期比－0.5%から同－0.6%に小幅に下方修正された。
企業部門では、財務省「法人企業統計」において季節調整済みの設備投資（ソフトウェアを含む・名目）が前期比
＋1.5%と増加したことから、GDP 統計の設備投資も前期比－1.2%から同－0.9%に上方修正された。民間在庫
は前回からほとんど変わらなかった。また、政府部門では、政府最終消費が前期比＋1.6%から同＋1.7%へ小幅
に上方修正された一方、公共投資は同－0.1%から同－0.5%へ下方修正された。なお、輸出、輸入ともに小幅に
下方修正されたが、外需寄与度に大きな修正はなかった。

（注）内需寄与度、民間在庫、外需寄与度は実質 GDP に対する寄与度（%ポイント）

### Page 2

2 / 2

 2 次速報値を踏まえた、2026 年 4～6 月期における需要項目別の動向（実質ベース）は以下の通り。

① 実質個人消費（前期比＋ 0.0%） 
 家計を取り巻く所得環境 は改善したものの （実質雇用者報酬は前期比＋ 1.0%）、イラン情勢の
悪化による消費者マインドの弱さもあり、 前期から横ばいとなった 。制度変更や価格上昇前の駆
け込みが一部に生じた影響もあって、 耐久財（前期比＋ 4.0% ）や半耐久財（同 ＋0.8% ）が増加
した一方、 非耐久財（ 同－1.9%）が加熱式たばこの値上げもあって落ち込んだほか、サービス
（同－0. 2%）も減少した。  
② 実質住宅投資（前期比－ 0.6%） 
 住宅投資に先行する住宅着工戸数は 2 四半期ぶりに増加したものの、床面積ベースでは伸び
悩んだこともあり 、住宅投資は 3 四半期ぶりに減少 した。 
③ 実質設備投資（前期比－ 0.9% ） 
 企業の投資意欲は良好なものの、建設 費をはじめ種々の 投資コスト が増加していることもあって 、
設備投資は 2 四半期連続で減少した。  
④ 実質民間在庫（前期比寄与度＋ 0.3%ポイント ） 
 2 月末からのイラン情勢悪化による供給制約懸念の高まりを受けて、メーカーが製品在庫 など
の積み増しを進めたことから、民間在庫はプラス 寄与となった。  
 なお、公的在庫は政府が石油備蓄の放出を進めたことから、前期比寄与度－ 0.5% ポイントとな
った。 
⑤ 実質政府最終消費（前期比＋1.7% ） 
 高校授業料無償化の拡充や小学校給食費の 無償化などもあり、 前期比で増加が続いた。  
⑥ 実質公共投資（前期比－ 0.5%） 
 名目では増加したものの、 工事コストの増加を受けた デフレーターの上昇に追いつ いておらず、
2 四半期ぶりに減少した 。 
⑦ 外需（前期比寄与度＋ 0.5%ポイント 、実質輸出：前期比＋ 0.4%、実質輸入：同－ 1.7%） 
 輸出は前期比＋ 0.4%と増加した。財はイラン情勢の悪化を受けて中東向けを中心に減少した
一方、サービスは研究開発サービスが一時的な要因により上振れたこともあって増加した。  
 輸入は前期比－ 1.7%と減少した。財は鉱物性燃料を中心に減少し、サービスもこのところ増加
が続いていた反動もあって減少した。  
 結果、輸出から輸入を差し引いた外需の寄与度は＋0.5% ポイントとプラスになった。

市場焦點
聯準會9月
升息一碼
• 聯邦基準利率升至 3.75%~4.00%，經濟預測部分上調2026-2028年GDP成長率、失業率皆下
調，通膨降溫進程仍相對緩慢 ，點陣圖顯示今年還有升息一碼的機會 ，這次升息對股票而言 ，
聯準會沒有準備以大幅犧牲經濟與就業換取通膨下降 ，降低了硬著陸風險；對債券而言 ，短
端仍須消化更高的政策利率，但長端反而可能受惠於抗通膨可信度改善。
AI模型
開發放緩
• Anthropic、OpenAI及xAI相繼支持放緩最先進AI模型開發速度。不過，前沿模型開發速度放
緩，並不代表AI基礎建設需求同步轉弱。晶片、算力、資料中心及能源等AI基礎建設仍處於供
不應求格局 ，短期若因市場情緒或資金槓桿調整而出現回檔 ，較可能反映估值與持倉修正 ，

而非AI需求基本面反轉 。後續若前期累積的槓桿壓力逐步消化 ，且全球總體經濟及地緣政治
風險未進一步惡化，AI供應鏈仍具中長期配置價值。
台灣央行
• 利率決議重點： (1)台灣央行宣布基準利率維持不變 ，重貼現率、擔保放款融通利率和短期融
通利率分別維持2%、2.375%和4.25%。(2)放鬆房市信用管制 ，調整自然人第2戶購屋貸款最
高成數上限，由6成調升為7成。(3)上修今年經濟成長率及通膨率 ，GDP上修至11.48%、CPI
及核心CPI分別上修至2.03%及2.16。(4)央行預估明年AI基礎建設需求將持續貢獻台灣出口表
現，不過由於基期已墊高，預測明年台灣經濟成長率為5.82%。

資料來源：Fed，華爾街見聞，2026.9.17
貨幣決議與9月、7月聲明稿差異，不再強調能源衝擊 全員支持升息
• 聯準會9月升息一碼，聯邦基準利率在3.75%~4.00%。
聲明稿重點差異
紅色字體為9月的新增部分，括號內藍色字體為刪除的7月聲明措辭
FOMC以12票贊成升息一碼 批准發布以下聲明 ：委員會決定 ，將聯邦基金利率目標 區間上調至 3.75%至4%維持在
3.50%至3.75%，以支持聯準會的雙重使命。委員會延續其維持銀行體系充足準備金的政策。
儘管中東衝突在一定程度上帶來了高水準的不確定性 ，美國經濟活動持續以穩健步調擴張 。儘管地緣政治情勢 等因素
使經濟前景仍籠 罩高度不確定性，但國內支出保持韌性，生產力成長強勁，企業資本投資也相當強勁穩健。新增就業

人數大致跟上勞動力成長速度，失業率也變化不大，顯示就業市場目前仍大致維持穩定。
相較於委員會2%的目標，通貨膨脹率依然偏高 ，這在一定程度上反映了部分特定部門 (包括能源)因供給衝擊所導致的
價格上漲。今日的政策行動，將有助於讓通膨更及時地回到2%目標，委員會將實現價格穩定。

聯準會會後記者會問答重點整理
1
通膨「過高且持續太久」 當
前重心偏向物價穩定
通膨數據未改善，通膨過高且持續太久，聯準會當前的政策重心偏向雙重使命中的物價穩定 。透過政策
行動表明，聯準會對抑制通膨是認真的。
2
經濟、就業與地緣政治促成
一致升息
近幾週公布的數據證實美國經濟正在走強 ，基礎經濟成長率高於先前判斷；其次 ，勞動市場依然穩健；
第三，通膨持續高於目標，地緣政治局勢也改變經濟展望 。華許表示，這三項因素共同促成當天「堅定
且一致」的決定。
3
點狀圖暗示今年再升 1碼 但
不是政策承諾
最新經濟預測顯示，多數官員認為今年仍有進一步升息空間。12 名官員預期年底前再升息 1 碼，另有 4
人預期還會升息 2 碼，僅 2 人主張利率維持在目前水準。不過，點狀圖反映的是官員對適當利率路徑的

個別預測，並非正式的承諾。
4
拒絕提供前瞻指引 不預判下
次會議結果
面對下一次升息可能落在 10 月或 12 月的追問，華許表示「我不給出前瞻指引。」
他強調，先前公開表達的是對貨幣政策紀律的承諾 ，而不是對特定利率決策的承諾 。聯準會不會預判未
來任何會議結果，後續行動仍將依據最新經濟數據及展望作出決定。
5
升息非市場逼迫 迴避川普施
壓問題
面對此次升息是否由債市波動及市場壓力所推動的提問，華許予以否認。
他表示是根據就業走勢、經濟強度及整體情勢作出決定 ，市場價格可作為觀察依據，但不能決定聯準會
的政策。
資料來源：Fed，華爾街見聞，2026.9.17

台灣利率連10凍 明年經濟展望佳
• 利率決議重點： (1)台灣央行宣布基準利率維持不變 ，重貼現率、擔保放款融通利率和短期融通利率分別維持 2%、2.375%
和4.25%。(2)放鬆房市信用管制，調整自然人第2戶購屋貸款最高成數上限，由6成調升為7成。(3)上修今年經濟成長率及通
膨率，GDP上修至11.48%(六月預估9.45 %)、CPI及核心CPI分別上修至2.03%及2.16%(六月預估1.91%及1.90%)。
• 觀點：全球AI資本支出維持上調的趨勢，台股供應鏈訂單直接受惠，央行預估明年AI基礎建設需求將持續貢獻台灣出口表現，
不過由於基期已墊高，預測明年台灣經濟成長率為5.82%。
• 台股短線展望：上週隨著聯準會利率決議公布後，台股量能回升，顯示短線消息面或已利空出盡。權值股台積電領軍台灣加

權指數站上月線，技術指標偏正向。投資佈局方面，由於地緣政治、通膨及貿易關稅風險仍存在不確定性，建議AI概念持股
核心布局行業龍頭企業，留意中小型成長股較易受到外部因素而股價大幅波動。
資料來源：中央銀行，2026.9.17。f代表央行預測數。

美國零售強勁回升擴大內需支撐，惟價格壓力與升息提高市場波動
• 零售全面回升，消費動能展現廣度：美國8月零售銷售月增1.2%，創3月以來最大增幅，優於市場預期，並扭轉7月修正
後月減0.5%；非實體店面銷售月增 2.6%、電子及家電增 1.6%，餐飲服務亦增 1.2%，且扣除汽車與汽油後仍增 1.2%，
控制組更增1.4%，顯示消費回升並非僅由汽車與燃料支出帶動，第三季內需仍具支撐。
• 名目消費偏強，通膨與升息增添需求壓力： 不過8月零售銷售年增6.0%屬名目增幅，部分反映物價上升因素；同期 CPI
年增3.4%，汽油價格月增3.9%，能源成本上升可能壓縮家庭實質購買力 ，加上Fed於9月決議升息1碼，後續利率政策
仍將視通膨與經濟數據調整，使市場焦點由消費回升轉向評估高利率與高物價對後續需求動能的影響。

• 後市觀察：短期零售回升仍有助支撐美國景氣，但若能源成本持續走高，加上升息後借貸成本上升，消費動能能否維持
將成為重要觀察點；投資配置可偏向美國投資級短中天期債券 ，兼顧收益與利率風險，股票則可聚焦獲利穩定、具定價
能力的大型優質企業，降低需求放緩與資金成本升高的影響。

顧 • AI安全監管升溫，短期壓抑市場情緒：Anthropic、OpenAI及xAI相繼支持放緩最先進AI模型開發速度，並引入獨立第三方
評估等安全機制，引發市場對AI資本支出降溫的疑慮。不過，前沿模型開發速度放緩，並不代表AI基礎建設需求同步轉弱。
Alphabet、微軟及亞馬遜近期AI相關積壓訂單持續攀升，合計達約1.64兆美元，其中微軟及Alphabet分別突破6,000億及
5,000億美元，搭配雲端業務持續成長，顯示企業AI需求仍具韌性，相關訂單有望逐步轉化為未來數年的營收與獲利。
• 槓桿壓力逐步消化，AI供應鏈仍具中長期配置價值：美國雲端巨頭擴大AI資本支出，壓抑短期自由現金流；相較之下，亞洲
科技企業自由現金流可望由目前約2,450億增至2028年的6,330億美元，支撐亞洲科技供應鏈中長期發展。短期若因市場情

緒或資金槓桿調整而出現回檔，較可能反映估值與持倉修正，而非AI需求基本面反轉。後續若前期累積的槓桿壓力逐步消化，
且全球總體經濟及地緣政治風險未進一步惡化，AI供應鏈仍具中長期配置價值。

油價在美國中期選舉前，或將維持在高檔
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.14
伊朗戰爭期間卡達油氣收入暴跌
卡達政府的石油和天然氣收入 美伊衝突
• 美伊衝突短期內仍難見明確解方，彭博預估雙方後續可能採取的行動，主要可分為以下四種情境：
(1)伊朗承受經濟封鎖：伊朗承受美國經濟制裁與施壓，避免進一步重大軍事升級。在美方護航行動支持下，商船仍可維持通行
荷姆茲海峽，國際原油供應未出現明顯中斷，油價預估在每桶80美元區間波動。
(2)伊朗將攻擊範圍限於海灣地區油輪與美軍：伊朗主要鎖定海灣地區能源運輸船隻及美軍目標，美方則採取有限度軍事回應，

衝突維持在一定範圍內，油價預估在每桶90美元區間波動。
(3)伊朗擴大攻擊範圍：攻擊目標不再局限於海灣地區船隻及美軍基地，海灣能源基礎設施以及紅海等重要航運咽喉，均可能成
為目標。局勢升級期間，油價可能維持在每桶100美元以上，待衝突緩和後回落至70至80美元區間，此情境發生的可能性最高。
(4)美伊雙方全面擴大開戰：伊朗進一步擴大攻擊目標，導致美國軍事資產及區域能源基礎設施遭受重大破壞，甚至不排除以色
列重新加入戰局，油價可能升至每桶120美元。

全球債市拋售勢頭不墜，AI融資競逐資金推升美債殖利率
• 長端殖利率走高，財政與通膨壓力仍是主因 ：近期美國長
天期公債殖利率持續走高，其中10年期美債殖利率更突破
5%，創2007年以來高點；除能源價格上升推升通膨疑慮
外，美國財政赤字、政府債務及持續性的發債需求 ，也提
高市場對長天期公債供給與期限溢酬的要求 ，反映長端利
率壓力仍未完全紓解。
• AI企業擴大融資，增加公司債供給與資金競逐： AI資料中
心、晶片與電力基礎建設的資本支出快速增加 ，帶動大型
科技企業提高債券融資規模；截至 6月，華爾街市場策略
研究機構Yardeni Research在追蹤外國私人投資人過去一
年買進美國公司債約3,900億美元，高於美國公債約3,290
億美元，顯示資金配置確有轉向公司債的現象 ，不過市場

研究亦指出，AI企業發債對美債殖利率的直接推升效果相
對有限，應可視為推動長天期利率上行的眾多因素之一。
• 後市觀察：若財政融資與AI基礎建設支出持續維持高檔 ，
長天期公債仍面臨較大的供給與資金配置壓力；但 AI投資
仍具中長期成長動能 ，配置上可關注資料中心 、半導體及
電力基礎建設供應鏈 ，固定收益則以短中天期美債及高品
質投資級公司債為主，降低長天期利率波動的影響。

新興市場本幣債券擁高票息提供防禦力
• 高票息提供防禦力：對債券投資人而言，票息就像安全墊。當市場利率上升、債券價格下跌時，持續收到的票息可以部分甚
至完全抵銷價格損失。新興市場本幣政府債券今年的表現，比市場原先預期更具韌性。目前新興市場本幣債券的持有收益約
為4.54%，已經足以對利率風險形成相當程度的保護 ，即使今年存續期間對多數市場造成負面影響 ，票息收益仍讓整體報酬
維持正數。這也是為什麼新興市場本幣債券在美伊衝突爆發後一度重挫超過 6%，但之後仍能逐步反彈 。哥倫比亞、巴西、
匈牙利及墨西哥的債券表現尤其突出。
• 市場已反映相當程度的升息風險：問題在於未來幾個月投資環境可能比上半年更加困難，能源價格上漲推升通膨，也迫使新
興市場央行維持較為鷹派的貨幣政策 。市場目前甚至預期 ，19個主要新興市場中 ，有18個市場的債券殖利率可能在年底前

上升。殖利率上升意味著債券價格承壓，因此投資人不能只看表面的高票息，必須同時評估未來利率變化可能造成資本損失。
新興市場本幣債券各因子對報酬率貢獻
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.15

新興市場本幣債券目前最大風險在於利率與匯率
• 存續期間仍是今年最主要的拖累因素：一般而言，債券的存續期間越高，當殖利率上升時，債券價格下跌的幅度就可能越大。
今年19個符合基準指數條件的新興市場中 ，有14個市場的總報酬受到存續期間拖累 ，其中韓國、馬來西亞、印尼、泰國及
菲律賓受到的負面影響最為明顯。更值得注意的是，新興市場債券的利率表現與美國公債高度相關，因此，投資新興市場債
券，不能只觀察當地央行，也必須密切留意美國聯準會利率政策及美國公債殖利率走勢。
• 匯率可能成為下一個重要變數：新興市場本幣債券除了債券價格本身，還存在另一項重要風險：匯率。今年即期匯率變動反
而對新興市場本幣債券帶來正面貢獻 ，約增加57個基點。但如果能源價格持續上漲並形成滯脹環境 ，順周期貨幣可能面臨

更大的貶值壓力 。目前市場預期年底前新興市場貨幣整體仍偏弱 ，19種新興市場貨幣中 ，有11種預計兌美元貶值 。其中土
耳其、哥倫比亞及巴西貨幣的潛在跌幅相對較大。這代表即使債券本身提供不錯的票息，如果當地貨幣大幅貶值，換算成美
元或其他投資人本國貨幣後，仍可能侵蝕甚至吞噬債券收益。
• 小結與投資建議：目前新興市場本幣債券比較像是高票息換取較高波動的投資，而不是單純追求資本利得的交易。在殖利率
已處於相對高檔、但利率與匯率風險仍未完全解除的情況下，採取分散市場、控制部位、逢回布局的策略會比追高更為合適。

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.15

• 多重資產配置可有效風險管理：美伊衝突持續及聯準會利率政策，使今年金融市場震盪加劇，加上第四季美國期中選舉到來，
增加市場更多不確定性，不過從歷史經驗來看，地緣政治事件對股市的影響多屬短期現象，市場在初期情緒性下跌後，通常會
逐步回歸基本面，相較於預測事件發展，投資人更應透過多元資產配置分散風險，以提升投資組合的穩定性與長期抗震能力。
• 透過多重資產廣納收益來源：在利率維持相對高檔的環境下，投資人更應重視收益來源的多元化，從下圖可見，相較於過去幾
年，掩護性買權、非投資等級債，及投資等級債等資產的收益率均顯著提升，提供投資組合更具吸引力的現金流來源，其中掩
護性買權收益率已超過10%，非投資等級債，及投資等級債亦分別達7%及5%以上水準，透過結合不同收益型資產，不僅有助

提升整體收益潛力，更能降低對單一市場或利率變動的依賴，在市場波動加劇時提高投資組合的穩定度與韌性，兼顧收益與長
期成長目標。

資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 上周三美國公布 8 月民間就業人數僅增加 3.8 萬人，低於市場預期的 4.8 萬人。
教育與醫療服務業新增 4.5 萬人，但製造業減少 1.7 萬人，專業與商業服務業
也出現萎縮 。 這反映出民間部門招聘動能放緩 ， 尤其是製造業與專業服務業的疲
弱。美元指數至高點回落 ， 惟上周五美國公布8 月非農就業人數新增 16.2 萬人 ，
遠超預期的 5.6 萬人 ，失業率維持在4.1%；勞動參與率回升至 61.6%， 平均工時
延長至 34.4 小時 ， 顯示勞動市場的韌性 。 雖然薪資增幅僅3.1%， 通膨壓力有限 ，
但強勁的就業數據仍強化了市場對美聯儲加息的預期 。 芝商所 FedWatch 工具顯
示，9 月加息機率已升至 62%，美元指數止跌回穩。
⚫ 紐西蘭央行上週三將官方隔夜拆款利率調升 25bps 至 2.75%， 並釋出緩步緊縮訊
號。央行聲明指出，逐步撤回貨幣政策刺激有助於通膨回到 2%目標中點，同時

降低未來大幅升息的必要性 。 總裁佈雷曼強調 ， 升息時機仍高度不確定 ， 短期核
心通膨可能續升 ， 但逐步退出寬鬆政策仍屬合宜 。 央行亦提醒經濟存在顯著下行
風險，政策將保持漸；然而消息公布後紐幣大跌，主因 2027 年利率預測較原先
預期低。
⚫ 加拿大央行上週三維持政策利率在 2.25%不變 ， 但警告通膨風險升高 。 美國新關
稅增加經濟不確定性 ， 或使企業延後投資與招聘 。 總裁麥克勒姆表示 ， 關稅對整
體經濟影響有限 ， 但中東戰事與高油價可能推升物價壓力 。 央行強調需警惕外部
因素對通膨的擴散效應，加拿大幣止跌回穩。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
美元兌新台幣匯率走勢
Sep/7/2026
＜上週走勢＞
上週台幣匯價高點為 31.590，低點在 31.827，以 31.630 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 0.2 分。
上週台幣先貶後升，週初，聯準會主席華許於全球央行年會發表的鷹派言論強
化市場對聯準會升息的預期，引發台灣市場股匯雙殺，外資匯出令台幣走貶；週二
早盤由於台股走升外資匯入，台幣一度走升，惟逢低進口商買美金力道強勁，加以
投信亦匯出，台幣由升轉貶，不過逢高出口商拋匯，台幣貶幅收斂；週三、周四，
中東戰事升溫，帶動美元指數走升，外資連續兩日賣超台股共計1390 億，匯出力道
強勁，台幣貶至周內低點 31.827，惟出口商拋匯需求及央行進場調節，抑制台幣貶
值空間；臨近周末，受益於美元指數走跌、亞幣走升，台幣止跌回穩，加以外資轉
向匯入，台幣收復上周大部分跌幅，終場在央行調節之下收在 31.630。
＜未來分析＞
預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場而言，美國勞動力市場尚屬穩健，非農就

業人數遠超預期，勞動參與率回升，令市場對於聯準會升息的預期有所回溫，將給
予美元指數支撐；就台灣市場來看，台灣 8 月製造業 PMI 54.7，顯示台灣製造業景
氣續呈大幅擴張，新增訂單持續暢旺，有利台幣表現，惟上週外資賣超台股並於外
匯市場匯出，將為台幣帶來貶值的壓力。綜合以上，預估本週台幣走勢區間震盪。
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.500-31.850

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Sep/7/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週人民幣震盪走升。週初，儘管華許鷹派言論提振美元指數，然而中國出口
商逢匯價反彈結售美金，帶動人民幣震盪走升，惟人民幣中間價升值速度放緩，顯
示監管單位在地緣政治風險再起的情況下，允許人民幣升值的意願有所降低，抑制
人民幣升值速度；周間由於人民幣已走升至年內高點附近，升值空間有限，日內人
民幣整體維持高檔窄幅盤整的格局 ， 週三晚間美國公布ADP 就業人數低於預期 ， 美
元指數走弱，人民幣受益走升；臨近周末人民幣開出中間價格為 6.7787，升破 6.78
整數大關並創三年半新高，加以日幣延續升值的態勢，同為亞幣的人民幣亦持續走
升，終場境外人民幣收在 6.7048，週線升值 264 點，境內人民幣則收在 6.7108，週
線升值 161 點。  
＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣區間偏升。國際市場方面，美國非農就業人數遠高預期，顯示
美國勞動力市場仍具韌性，加以美伊地緣政治風險近來有升溫的現象，油價上彈再
度引發通膨升溫的擔憂，市場仍持續定價聯準會九月升息的可能性，美元指數有望
獲得支撐。中國國內方面，人行中間價格接連兩日創近三年新高，釋放人民幣偏強
信號， 支撐人民幣延續升值的方向 。 技術面方面，USD/CNH 匯價居於各天期均線下
方，且均線向下發散，加以匯價有效跌破 6.7150 關鍵支撐位，空方動能較盛，顯示
人民幣將延續升值的態勢。綜合以上，預估本週人民幣走勢偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6800-6.7300

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/

美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Sep/7/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 USD/JPY 先上後下。週初，因美伊不確定性再次升溫，油價上漲推升通膨
預期，美元自近期的低點反彈，帶領 USD/JPY 上升到 160 上方；週間，市場對日本
官方可能採取支撐匯率措施的預期升溫，且傳出日本央行可能在近期會議上宣布升
息及加快正常化步伐，帶動日圓連續兩天大幅走升；臨近週末，美元指數止穩於年
線附近，USD/JPY 跌深反彈，終場收在 156.24，週線大跌 381 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間偏空震盪，主要原因如下：一、日圓上週飆升，數據證
實非官方干預，主因日銀審議委員高田創釋出鷹派信號，市場押注 9 月升息機率達
75%，疊加聯準會理事沃勒釋放按兵不動傾向 ，美日利差有望縮窄推升日圓。二 、美
國 8 月非農新增就業達 16.2 萬人遠超預期，帶動9 月升息機率上升至 62%，並強化
年底前升息兩次之押注。儘管川普發出中斷貿易威脅強推降息，但在就業穩健與主
席華許偏鷹派立場下，決策焦點全面轉向本週即將公布 CPI 數據，美日利差及油價
壓力預計將限縮 USD/JPY 下行空間。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY 在日
央加速升息的預期下急跌，然匯價於 155 整數關卡止跌，若能有效跌破則可能還有
一波較大的跌幅。綜合以上，預估USD/JPY 本週呈現區間偏空震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：154.00-158.00

美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
         Sep/7/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 EUR/USD 震盪走升。週初，歐元跌深反彈，不過在美伊不確定性再次升
溫及美國利率維持偏高路徑的定價支撐美元需求下，EUR/USD 弱勢整理；週間，市
場在等待美國就業相關數據公布，歐元持續走落，最低來到 1.1565；臨近週末，美
國就業數據好於預期，但聯準會官員鴿派言論，加上川普發言使升息預期受抑制，
帶動歐元先下後上，終場收在 1.1613，週線上漲 33 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、美國 8 月非農就業增長超
預期，帶動交易員對聯準會本月重啟升息的押注顯著升溫。對此，美國總統川普上
週五強烈抨擊高利率使美國陷入劣勢，揚言若聯準會不調降借貸成本，美國將直接
停止與對美享有貿易順差的國家進行貿易 ， 意圖以極端貿易禁令迫使央行寬鬆。二、
受能源推動，歐元區 8 月通膨升至 3.3%，但核心通膨降至 2.4%，顯示油價上漲未
出現進一步滲透至工資、終端服務定價等長期通膨預期的二輪效應。歐洲央行管委
科赫示警通膨上行風險，強化 9 月升息預期，短線上為歐元提供支撐。三、技術面
而言 ， 在日線圖中 ，EUR/USD 匯價在多次向下測試 1.1550 的關鍵價位均獲得支撐 ，
目前承壓於年線但獲得月線支撐。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈現區間震盪的
可能性較高。

                         新台幣利率走勢分析

115.08.31-115.09.04 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30 天期短票利率   1.66% ~ 1.68% 
公債－10 年期-A15106   1.90% 
－ 5 年期- A15108   1.82%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分，本週邁入新的提存期，因應 9 月有升息預期及季節性用
款因素 ， 銀行間調度偏保守 ，惟整體市場資金供需仍屬平穩， 令短率持穩於近期
區間。 
週四央行標售 364 天期定存單 1200 億元 ， 加權平均得標利率為1.643%， 符合
市場預期區間 1.64%-1.66%，較上期標售結果上升 1.7 個基本點。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.66%~1.68%，利率大致持平於上週。 
二、 本週市場交投清淡，5 年期指標券 A15108 週二搓合成交在 1.82%，10 年期指
標券 A15106 則未成交，最近成交利率為 1.90%。 
週二標售 5 年期公債得標利率 1.82%， 落在市場預期區間1.80%~1.82%上緣 ，
投標倍數 1.57 倍為 2024 年 11 月以來最低 ， 顯示在國際債市氣氛偏空影響下 ，
市場投標態度謹慎。 
韓國、日本央行處於升息循環階段、聯準會主席 Warsh 暗示若 PCE 未往 2%
目標邁進將有升息可能 ， 在台灣經濟成長與通膨維持高位下 ， 台灣央行面臨緊
縮壓力 ， 加上美伊戰事再度升溫 ， 美債殖利率將呈震盪偏升格局 ， 預期台債殖
利率將隨美債走勢維持高檔盤整，預估 5 年券區間為 1.80%~1.85%，10 年券
區間為 1.90%至 1.95%。

3Q26 Results Miss on Lower

Largan reported 3Q26 net income of NT$5.182bn, down 27% YoY.
The company reported a 11% YoY revenue decline, to NT$15.651bn; gross margin
declined YoY and QoQ to 42.2% in 3Q26, lower than expected due to lower new-
project yields and structural green electricity cost hikes.
Management expects shipments to be flat MoM in October and decline

sequentially in November as order momentum is weaker-than-expected.
FAU/CPO development well on track: First FA  project passed customer
verification; expanding from FA into FAU, including coupling and potential FAU-to-
carrier assembly, with a dedicated new facility expected to support mass production
from around mid-2027. We maintain our long-term positive view on Largan's FAU/
CPO opportunities.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Largan Precision (3008.TW)
Base case: Our price target is based on an SOTP valuation, comprising the traditional business
and potential FAU opportunity. We value the traditional business using a residual income
model, consistent with our Greater China Hardware Tech coverage, assuming an 8.5% cost of
equity, a 7% net profit CAGR over 2026-36E and a 3% terminal growth rate. We separately

value the FAU business, assuming a 40% market share in 2028.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected high-end smartphone demand
n Faster-than-expected penetration of periscopes
n Stronger-than-expected CPO volume growth
Risks to Downside
n Greater-than-expected competition, which would erode Largan's ASP and gross margin
n Weaker-than-expected smartphone demand, especially for high-end models
n Market share gains and more pricing pressure from Sunny

n Weaker-than-expected CPO volume growth

물량 확대는 증익으로 귀결

주가 추이

견조한 판매 확대로 매출 견조, 이익은 시장 기대치 하회 예상 
3Q26 동사 매출액과 영업이익은 각각 33.6조원(+17.3% 이하 Y oY)과 2.39조
원(+63.6%) 기록 전망. 3Q 매출액은 친환경차 중심 신차 확대 통해 두 자릿수 
성장세를 기록. 3Q 판매량은 도매 기준 84.3만대(+7.6% 이하 Y oY)로 전년 동
기 대비 성장 폭을 확대했고, 특히 친환경차 판매량 29.8만대(+45.9%)를 기록
하며 이번 분기 매출 성장에 절대적으로 기여.  
다만, 3Q 수익성은 4~5월 상승했던 원자재 투입 영향과 ‘경제형EV 믹스’가 더
욱 확대(13.2% → 14.5%)되면서 단기 수익성에 부정적 작용하며 시장 기대치 
2.5조원을 다소 하회할 전망. 그러나, 기말 환율이 1,356원/$ 수준으로 낮 아 지
면서 전년 동기 수 익 성 악화에 크게 영 향 을 미쳤던 판매보증비 외화환산손익 
영향이 개선(1.6%p 수준) 기대되며, 관세 부담 또한 북미 HEV 확대로 전년 동
기 대비 약 4,500억원(2.0%p) 감소하면서 부정적 수익성 영향 만회 전망.  
유럽은 점유율 확대, 북미는 수익성 개선으로 
올해 북미 HEV와 유럽 BEV 중심 신차 출시 및 물량 확대가 본격화되면
서, ’27년에는 이익 성장 기여도가 확대될 것으로 전망. 미국 조 지 아 텔 루 라 이
드 HEV 신규 생산(2.8만대) 및 HMGMA 스 포 티 지HEV신규 생산(1.0만
대) 등 수 요 가 높은 북미 현 지 에 서 HEV 물량 확대 중으로 4Q에도 텔루라이
드 HEV와 스포티지 HEV의 현지 생산/판매 확대를 비롯하여 셀토스 HEV 
출시로 ’27년 북미 중심 HEV 신차 물량 증가 및 믹스 개선 기대. 유럽은 강한 
BEV 수요가 지속되면서 중국 업체들의 공세 강화 중이나 동사의 물량 확대 또
한 견조. 3Q EV3 2.9만대, EV5 2.2만대 등 유럽 BEV 수요 대응 전략 주효. ’27
년에는 K4/셀토스 및 쏘렌토 등 HEV 신차 중 심 의 친환경차 PT 믹스 다양화
로 유럽 내 물량 확대 흐름 지속 전망 
물량 확대는 증익으로 귀결, 기말 배당 매력도 부각 
’27년 동사 영업이익 추 정 치 는 11.1조원(+13.5%)으로 증익 지속 전망. 최근 
환율 추세 고려 시 내년 수익성에 부 정 적 영향 확대 가능성 존재하나, 올해 확
인된 신차 중심 물량 확대를 통해 부정적 환율 영향을 상쇄할 것 으 로 기대. 
12M EPS 추정치 하향(-2.9%) 및 상 반 기 적 용 한 코스피 프리미엄 제거(-
10%)로 목 표 주 가 를 260,000원으로 하향. 그러나, RA 설립에서의 동사 지분 
참여 및 기말 배당(’26년 DPS 7,000원 추정)에 따른 상 대 적 배당 수익 매력
도 부각 등은 주가 모 멘 텀 에 있어 기대 가능한 요소.

이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 8

기아 (000270) [한화리서치]

8 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

## Document 176: August CPI – Came in on our expectations (2026-10-13)

Source PDF: all report pdf/August CPI – Came in on our expectations (2026-10-13).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-13
Pages: 16
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #australia #rba #economicbulletinsaustralia #inflation
August CPI – Came in on our expectations
By Justin Smirk & Neha Sharma
15:27 September 30 2026 Share
August Monthly CPI: Headline 0.4%mth/4.0%yr, Trimmed Mean 0.2%mth/3.6%yr. Market
services ex volatile –0.4%mth/3.3%yr.

The CPI rose 0.4% in August, spot on Westpac’s expectation but a bit softer than market
expectations of 0.5%. Due to base effects the annual pace picked up to 4.0%yr from 3.5%yr.

### Page 2

Key factors driving the headline result were auto fuels (14.8%); games, toys & hobbies (10.2%) with
rents providing a solid base (0.3%) while some offset was provided by both domestic (–4.9%) and
international holiday travel & accommodation (–1.5%).
Trimmed mean (TM) inflation printed 0.2% again bang on Westpac’s expectation while the market
was slightly higher at 0.3%. The annual pace was flat at 3.6%yr, the third month in a row at this pace,
while the six-month annualise pace was steady at 3.9%yr.

Key factors driving the TM print were: rents (0.3%) and new dwelling purchases (0.2%). The TM
distribution ranged from –0.38% to 0.82%.
Today’s inflation update revealed price discounting for some durable goods as retail demand softens.
We would expect to see a bounce from these categories in the following month. There was also
offsetting strength elsewhere in durable good so at least for now, the momentum continues to
suggest some near term upside risk to our current inflation estimates.

Nevertheless, as the TM came in on expectations and based on a first quick pass at updating the
quarterly profile, we see little pressure to revise our current Q3 estimates of 1.3%qtr for the CPI and
0.9%qtr for the TM.
The RBA remains alert to near term inflationary risks. We are expecting the momentum in TM
inflation to remain well above the top of the target band through to year-end underpinning our
expectations for the RBA to raise rates again in November.

### Page 3

August CPI in more detail
Following the bumper read in July, the monthly CPI moderated in August rising 0.4%mth. This was in
line with our expectations and slightly softer than the market consensus. This took the year-ended pace
to 4.0%yr. On a seasonally adjusted basis the CPI rose 0.7%mth highlighting the impact of seasonal
sales in August.

They key drivers behind the monthly lift were automotive fuel, up 14.8%mth, contributing 0.5ppts to the
headline figure. The surge resulted from the ending of the fuel excise discount on August 3 and rising
global energy prices. With crude oil prices still elevated due to the conflict in the middle east, and
growing shortages of refined products due to both the middle east conflict and Ukrainian assaults on
Russian refining capacity, auto fuel is expected to remain an inflationary impulse at least until the end
of 2026.

The monthly TM also softened, rising 0.2%mth compared to the 0.5%mth read from July. The result
was again in line with our expectations, and very close at two decimal places (0.24%mth vs
0.23%mth), but softer than the market's view. The year-ended pace held at 3.6%yr for the third
consecutive month, although it continued to ease marginally at the second decimal place. In contrast,
the six-month annualised pace has continued to trend higher since April and is currently running at
3.9%yr.

### Page 4

There is a possibility that as the ABS continues to grapple with seasonal adjustment issues we will see
revisions to the TM estimates. Last July also saw a strong 0.48%mth read which eased to 0.29%mth in
August. This is not too dissimilar from the 0.48%mth and 0.24%mth reads for 2026.

We are expecting core inflation to start to moderate as we get to year end but our current estimate for
the month of December still has the TM running at a 3.3% in the year while the six-month annualised
pace is still a chunky 3.1%. Applying a trim on the seasonally adjusted 3m/3m expenditure classes
yields a TM of 0.9%qtr currently. This is in line with our current Q3 TM estimate.

Other measures of underlying inflation told a similar story, easing on a monthly basis but holding steady
on a year-ended basis. Market services ex volatiles fell –0.4%mth to be 3.3%yr higher. Excluding both
volatiles & holidays saw a 0.2%mth/4.1%yr lift.

Most market services components eased in the month (including on a seasonally adjusted basis) but
annual growth across this group as a whole remained above the RBA's 2-3% target band. The near-
term momentum (three-month annualised) is also quite strong across several components of services.
Of note include deposit and loan facilities (11.2%mth), sports participation (10.9%), clothing repair
(7.0%), restaurant meals (6.8%) and other household services (5.2%). We are closely watching market
services as this is where inflationary risks, outside of energy, appear to be most persistent.

Due to its sizable weight in the CPI, and the fact price changes here tend to be less volatile than many
other categories, so they almost always appear in the TM estimates, housing prices remain a key
focus. Rents rose 0.3%mth in August, in line with our expectations, to be up 3.6%yr. Since April, the
seasonally adjusted rents series has been running lower than the non-seasonally adjusted series. We
are expecting rents to hold around this pace till year end before moderating modestly as we move
through 2027.

New dwellings inflation was 0.2%mth in August (softer than our expectation for a 0.4%mth rise). Since
peaking in May, monthly price growth has slowed in each successive month, potentially reflecting
increasing difficulty for developers in passing through higher costs amid softer conditions in established
housing markets and weaker demand in the face of higher interest rates. The annual pace looks to
have topped out. It was at 5.8%yr in June easing to 5.4%yr currently with the three-month annualised
pace at 3.7%. We expect dwelling price inflation to continue easing through to the end of 2027 as
falling established house prices limit the ability of builders to pass on rising construction costs. There is
a medium-term risk that as conditions improve builders may raise prices more aggressively to claw
back margins.

### Page 5

Overall, the breadth of inflation moderated in August. The share of the basket running above 3%yr
eased from 45% to 42%, with the share below 2%yr increasing from 38% to 41%.

### Page 6

Key surprises in the month:
Upside surprises

Downside surprises
Insurance surprised to the upside (0.7%mth vs 0.3%mth) after a run of weaker-than-usual reads over
the past two months.
Motor vehicle repair costs rose a solid 0.8% against our 0.3%mth expectations, with spare parts and
accessories also up 0.3%mth vs –0.4%mth.
Footwear rose 1.7%mth against our expectations for a -2.9%mth decline, the gains were broad-
based across women, men & children.
Household goods surprised to the upside. Household appliances, utensils and tools rose 1.9%mth
against our flat expectations, meanwhile household textiles fell –1.0%mth against our expectations
for a –5.0%mth fall. Furniture and furnishings also fell by less than expected –0.6%mth vs –1.3%mth.
Recreation and culture surprised to the upside (–1.0%mth vs –1.5%mth), owing to a 1.4%mth lift in
other recreation, sport & culture against our expectations for a flat read on the back of an outsized
gain in games, toys & hobbies (10.2%mth vs 2.5%mth). Holiday travel was marginally less weak than
expected (–3.3% vs –3.5%mth).

### Page 7

Food was the key downside miss falling –0.1%mth against our expectations for a 0.2%mth lift. Meat
& seafood (–0.9%mth vs 0.5%mth) and fruit & vegetables (–1.2%mth vs 0.1%mth) were the key
areas of weakness.
Audio, visual and computing equipment and services came in weaker-than-expected (0.7%mth vs
1.2%mth), owing to a softer 0.6%mth lift in computing services (3.0%mth expected). This was partly
balanced by a stronger than expected lift in computing equipment (0.9%mth vs –0.5%mth). The latter

is where we are most likely to see the impact of the AI boom in the CPI, due to computer chip
inflation, other than construction cost inflation for new dwellings.
Motor vehicles came in lower at –0.4%mth vs –0.1%mth which could be the start of price pressures
associated with the rapid expansion of the share of Chinese cars in the Australian market.
Automotive fuel was also slightly softer at 14.8%mth vs 16.7%mth.
Garments and accessories were both softer. Garments fell –1.9%mth against our expectations for a

0.2%mth rise, meanwhile accessories came in at 0.1%mth vs 0.5%mth expected.

### Page 8

What does this mean for the RBA?
Yesterday, post the RBA rate hike and monetary policy decision statement, Westpac changed its view
from a November follow up rate hike being a risk to being the base case. The post-meeting statement
was notable for its emphasis on the Middle East conflict, noting that “global energy prices are now
much higher than had been assumed in the August forecasts”. In addition, RBA’s liaison suggests firms
are passing this on or planning to do so. The pass-through was characterised as partial, and additional

to domestically driven inflation arising from capacity pressures. We read this as implying that the RBA
is concerned about upside risks to underlying inflation in Q4 of this year, as well as Q3.

### Page 9

Related articles
Our current forecast of 0.9%qtr for the September quarter TM stands post today’s release and
represents a step up from 0.8% in the June quarter holding the annual pace at 3.6%yr. Our current
forecast for the Q4 TM is 0.7%qtr/3.3%yr but given the move in energy prices, and anecdotes of firms
now passing on those rising costs, we too see upside risks to this estimate.

As such, we hold to the view that the RBA will follow up with another rate hike in November. For more
information on the RBA decision please see Luci Ellis’s note “RBA hikes in September, November
follow-up now expected".

## Document 201: Cliff Notes_ mulling the minutes (2026-10-13)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ mulling the minutes (2026-10-13).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-13
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: mulling the minutes
By Sian Fenner, Ryan Wells & Illiana Jain
11:10 October 09 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
The only data release of note in Australia this week was the October Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment
Survey, which reported a 4.7% drop in the headline index. At 80.4, sentiment has tumbled back to
deeply pessimistic levels, similar to the post-pandemic cost-of-living shock when readings were among

the weakest in the survey’s more than fifty-year history. Indeed, inflation and interest rate pressures are
once again clearly front of mind for consumers, with both the view on ‘family finances vs a year ago’

### Page 2

and ‘family finance next 12 months’ falling 8.0% and 6.4% in the month, respectively. Moreover, the
RBA’s September rate hike looks to have had a significant impact – sentiment tanked 20% between
those surveyed before the RBA decision and those surveyed afterwards.

Views on the economic outlook were relatively more resilient, but they still weakened, with the one-year
and five-year ahead sub-indexes falling 1.6% and 0.4% respectively. Consumers are becoming more
‘on edge’ about job security, albeit not outright alarmed. The unemployment rate remains relatively low
versus history, although it has drifted up to a cycle high of 4.6% as labour supply continues to outstrip
demand. We expect it to hold there in next week’s September Labour Force Survey – see our preview
for more detail. All told, with higher interest rates, renewed inflation pressures and a softening jobs
market all weighing at once, households are entering the final months of the year on the back foot.

Offshore, the focus was on US data.

Before turning to this week's releases, it is worth noting that September non-farm payrolls came in
softer than expected across most metrics. Employment rose by only 29k (89k expected), and there
were sizeable downward revisions to prior months, with a combined 60k removed from July and August
payrolls, leaving gains of just 31k and 29k respectively. The details pointed to weaker hiring in leisure &
hospitality and education & health services industries, although construction and manufacturing
employment continued to benefit from ongoing data-centre-related investment. The unemployment rate
edged up to 4.2%, with softer employment occurring alongside a rise in labour force participation to
61.8%. Meanwhile wage growth moderated to 0.1%mth (3.0%yr). Taken together, the report suggests
labour demand is cooling, with the three-month average pace of job creation slowing to around 50k.
However, softer payroll gains are not necessarily indicative of labour market deterioration. Lower
immigration and ageing demographics have reduced the pace of employment growth needed to keep
the unemployment rate stable. As such, the report is more consistent with a labour market that is
gradually normalising than one entering a meaningful downturn.

The September FOMC minutes reinforced a distinctly hawkish message, with participants noting they
had "not seen sufficient progress on lowering inflation in recent months" and expressing concern that
inflation risks remained "skewed to the upside". Policymakers highlighted that both core services and
core goods inflation remained elevated, with some attributing persistent goods price strength to the
effects of the AI buildout despite the sector's traditional disinflationary role. There was also concern
that, after more than five years of inflation above target, elevated inflation could begin to influence
inflation expectations and wage- and price-setting behaviour. In contrast, labour market conditions
were generally viewed as stable and "close to maximum employment". While a majority noted the
labour market had strengthened somewhat recently, several participants highlighted unusually low
dynamism, characterised by low hiring, low layoffs, a low job-finding rate and elevated long-term
unemployment. Importantly, participants generally judged that aggregate wage growth was moderate
and "consistent with inflation moving toward 2 percent", with several explicitly noting that "the labor
market was not currently a source of inflationary pressures".

### Page 3

Related articles
A key focus was the interaction between AI investment, productive capacity and inflation. While
participants broadly expected AI-related investment to boost productivity growth and potential output
over time, several warned that "the AI buildout could cause aggregate demand to outpace aggregate
supply over the medium term, putting upward pressure on inflation". Consistent with this view, some
participants had raised their estimate of the neutral policy rate and several judged the current policy
stance to be "not restrictive or only mildly restrictive". Against this backdrop, all participants supported
the September rate increase, and most assessed that another increase in the federal funds rate would
likely be appropriate by year-end, although future decisions would remain data dependent.

The September ISM Services PMI released this week painted a similar picture of an economy that
remains resilient but is gradually losing momentum. The headline index eased to 54.9 from 55.4 in
August, with the decline driven by a sharp fall in business activity and a 9.4pt drop in new export
orders, which slipped into contractionary territory for the first time in eight months. Labour market
conditions improved modestly with the employment index moving to broadly neutral territory 50.1 after
two months below 50, supported by elevated backlogs and still-solid demand. Respondent
commentary illustrated the mixed nature of labour demand, with some firms reporting they were "filling
positions vacated due to promotions or retirements", while others cited "restructuring due to efficiencies
gained using AI tools". Cost pressures also remained elevated, with the prices paid index rising to 74.0,
its highest level since July 2022 and around 18pts above its historical average, while fuel costs, tariffs
and supply chain constraints remained the most frequently cited concerns. Overall, the report suggests
services activity remains consistent with ongoing expansion, albeit at a slower pace, while labour
demand and pricing pressures remain firm.

Overall, the data supports our view that the FOMC will move to a broadly neutral policy stance by the
end of the year with one more rate hike, although the case for a back-to-back move in October has
become less clear cut.

Wired for growth
Initiate coverage with an Outperform rating and target price of NT$270
ACES is entering a two-engine growth phase. It is a key power cable supplier to
Bloom Energy as Bloom doubles its fuel-cell capacity, and it is expanding its high-
speed connector content from AI server daughterboards to ASIC motherboards, as

a high-speed cable leg comes on stream. We forecast a 72% EPS Cagr over 2026-
28, with OPM expanding from 7.4% in 2026 to 11.0% in 2027. Our TP of NT$270
is based on 20x 2H27-1H28SP EPS, and we initiate coverage at Outperform.
Bloom Energy the key earnings driver in 2027
ACES's multiyear partnership with Bloom locks in its share as Bloom plans to lift
capacity from 2GW in 2026 to 4GW/6GW in 2027/28, based on our checks. ACES
has shipped fuel-cell power cables to Bloom since 1Q26 and will add external DLO

high-voltage cables, with ASP of US$80-100k per set. We expect Bloom power
cables to be NT$5.8bn of sales and account for 28% of group revenue in 2027.
Power products need little incremental R&D and benefit from strong operating
leverage. We expect overall OPM to expand from 7.4% in 2026 to 11.0% in 2027.
High-speed products a new driver
Previously, ACES supplied only daughterboard connectors, at 10-20 units per rack.
On ASIC platforms, where ODMs have more say in supplier selection, ACES has

secured the opportunity to ship both motherboard and daughterboard connectors
(about 30 units per rack) and broadens its connector product offerings. With Gen6
connectors carrying around a 15% ASP premium over Gen5, and ACES as one of
only four vendors qualified by CSPs at Gen6, we forecast server revenue growth of
107%/74% YoY in 2026/27. The newly added Gen6 PCIe cable product, qualified
by two CSPs, began shipping in April 2026 at 20% of GM, with room to rise towards

30% as yield improves. Hence, we expect GM to recover to 26% in 2027.
Capacity in place ahead of demand
ACES has committed firm capacity ahead of demand. For power cables, it has leased
a new facility next to its Philippine plant, with new capacity planned to come online
in 4Q26. For server products, it plans to raise connector capacity from 4m to 5m
units/mo and will expand high-speed cable production lines from two to eight.
Initiate coverage with an Outperform rating

We forecast 2027/28SP EPS of NT$11.19/NT$14.93, with our 2027 forecast 48%
ahead of consensus. Our TP of NT$270 is based on 20x 2H27-1H28SP EPS, backed
by a 72% EPS Cagr in 2026-28SP driven by power cable ramp-up and high-speed
connector shipment growth. We initiate coverage with an Outperform rating.

Wired for growth ACES - O-PF

Bloom Energy the key earnings driver in 2027
We see tremendous advantages for ACES from Bloom Energy’s strong demand backed
by its aggressive expansion plan. ACES supplies inner power cables used inside each
fuel cell in 2026 and will begin supplying external diesel locomotive (DLO) cables that
connect fuel cell stacks into a fuel cell module (energy server). Each set (including inner
cable and external cable) carries dollar content of US$80-100k.

Furthermore, ACES has worked with Bloom on high-voltage power cables for SOFC
for around six years, even though the real contribution did not transpire until 2026. On
the back of this long-standing relationship, ACES is the key supplier during the
development phase and holds at least a 30% share in the mass production stage.
Together with these considerations, we expect inner cables to contribute revenue of
NT$5,113m/NT$8,074m in 2027/28, while external cables will add revenue of

NT$707m/NT$1,154m.
Figure 1
Power cable revenue from Bloom Energy and YoY change
Source: SinoPac
As power scarcity remains the key bottleneck for AI datacentre construction, Bloom
serves as a suitable solution and offers fuel cell systems that generate electricity from
natural gas. Its modular, on-site, 24/7 power platform stands apart in the power sector.
Against a conventional grid built on centralised generation, Bloom delivers compelling
time-to-power, cleaner air and water emissions profile, native-DC compatibility with

emerging 800V AI datacentre architectures, and a solution that communities and
nearby residents accept more readily.

As of year-end 2025, Bloom's product backlog grew about 140% YoY to roughly

Wired for growth ACES - O-PF
6 October 2026 6
infrastructure, with Bloom providing its fuel cell-based power generation systems and
Brookfield providing capital and infrastructure development capabilities. The strategic
financing framework expanded on June 30, 2026, from the original $5bn (October
2025) to $25bn, a fivefold increase. On customer wins, Nebius dropped its previously
planned gas and combustion turbines in May 2026 and switched to Bloom’s SOFC

solution, signing a 10-year master agreement worth up to $2.6bn. The first phase
covers 328MW of installed capacity, or roughly 250MW of guaranteed capacity, and
is expected to come online within 2026. Bloom also expanded its Oracle contract from
an initial 1.2GW to up to 2.8GW and signed deals including AEP (up to 1 GW) and
Equinix (over 100 MW across 19 datacentres), broadening its customer base materially.
Wide range of power cable products
ACES supplies Bloom with a variety of power cables across different specifications,

including Power Conversion System (PCS) cables, hot box cables and Diesel
Locomotive (DLO) high-voltage cables, rather than a single product type. In 2026,
ACES only supplies PCS cables and hot box cables, while the company will expand its
product offerings to DLO high-voltage cables from 2027, which we expect to
contribute another NT$707m of sales for the whole year.
The PCS cables and hot box cables are the inner power cables. Hot box cables
interconnect individual fuel cells within the hot box, where power generation creates

significant heat and therefore requires materials with higher durability and heat
resistance. Kaori and MTAR supply hot boxes for fuel cell power generation systems,
which require ACES’s hot box cables as one of their key components.
On the other hand, PCS cables are installed inside the Power Conversion System (PCS)
and are supplied to AcBel, a PCS solution provider. The PCS converts DC output into
usable power and manages load changes. As SOFCs generate DC power with voltage

varying according to load, the output cannot be directly connected to a datacentre.
The external power cable portfolio is mainly DLO high-voltage cables, which transmit
high-voltage DC power between fuel cell platforms, power modules and microgrid
systems. As external cables need to withstand higher voltage levels, their dollar content
is expected to be slightly higher than that of inner power cables.
Lastly, ACES also ships cables directly to Bloom Energy for development purposes.

DLO high-voltage cable opportunity heading into 800V DC era
We also see DLO cables, which are currently used outside the fuel cell, as having
significant potential after introducing 800V architecture. Under conventional AC
architecture, grid power, onsite turbines and reciprocating engines supply AC

power, and the downstream transformer, switchgear and UPS cables belong to the
utility/EPC supply chain, which ACES does not address. Bloom can deliver native
DC power directly to the rack, so the DC output cables might become part of
Bloom’s system. We see this opening as a new content opportunity for ACES as a
qualified high-voltage power cable supplier.
Within Bloom’s energy server under 800V architecture, the longer series stack of
fuel cells and the replacement of the inverter with DC-DC conversion require a

redesign of the internal harness to high-voltage specifications, which we believe
positions ACES to ship a growing volume of high-voltage DLO cables over time.

In 3Q24, ACES passed product qualification with ODMs for the high-speed
connectors used on Nvidia GB200 networking cards (NICs) and accelerator cards,

supply chain. In 2025, server revenue accounted for 10% of ACES’s total revenue.
Looking ahead, we expect ACES’s server business to grow 107% in 2026, thanks to

its new product offerings, higher content per rack of high-speed products, and
strong contribution from two major ASIC customers. Looking ahead, with the new
connector capacity coming online and increasing ASP driven by connector spec
upgrade, we expect server revenue to further grow 74%/33% in 2027/28SP.

Broadening connector footprint in ASICs
Since Nvidia has strict control over its supply chain, ACES previously supplied only
the daughterboard high-speed connectors for GB200/300 servers, at roughly 10 to
20 units per rack. On ASIC platforms, since ODMs have greater influence over
supplier selection, ACES secures share on both the motherboard and the
daughterboard, lifting content to around 30 units per rack.

Besides, ACES historically supplied only MCIO connectors to these ASIC customers,
but as further qualifications clear, the company will broaden its connector product
offerings in 2026 to include Multi-Track, board-to-board, and E1/E3 connectors.
We expect the contribution from ASIC customers to scale further in 2H26, driving
server revenue to grow 64% HoH.

Riding the Gen6 ASP curve with a compatibility advantage
Aside from the spectacular growth in the connector shipments, we expect ACES to
benefit from the ongoing Gen6 connector upgrade cycle, with ASPs expected to
increase by 15%. The AI server trays currently adopt a mix of Gen5 and Gen6
connectors, but we expect the penetration of Gen6 connectors to gradually
increase going forward as the migration from Gen5 to Gen6 is the first major

generational shift since Genoa introduced Gen5 in 2022.

### Page 12

Wired for growth ACES - O-PF
6 October 2026 12
Figure 15
Gen6 connector ASP is 15% higher than Gen5
Source: SinoPac
Only three to four connector vendors are qualified by CSPs at Gen6, and ACES is
one of them. Furthermore, ACES can supply both Gen5 and Gen6 platforms under
its existing qualification, with no need to re-sample or re-qualify for Gen5. This is
because Gen6 connectors keep footprint backward compatibility with Gen5. By
contrast, other peers must re-sample and run through the full qualification process

again to move up to Gen6.
About the PCIe migration and Gen6 spec upgrade
PCIe has long been used for on-board and in-chassis interconnects. In the Vera
Rubin platform, PCIe Gen6 serves as the in-tray bridge to front-of-tray modules
such as CX-9 SuperNICs, BlueField-4 DPUs and NVMe storage. GPU-to-GPU scale-
up runs on NVLink, with UALink as the open alternative; these fabrics work
alongside PCIe.
In order to address ever-larger training models, the PCIe must scale with the fast-

growing size of model parameters and datasets. The move from Gen5 to Gen6 is
not a simple speed doubling, even though per-lane bandwidth rises from 32 GT/s
to 64 GT/s. It reworks the link from the coding scheme up. Gen1 through Gen5
doubled I/O speed using NRZ signaling. Gen6 switches to PAM4 four-level
signaling and, for the first time, adds FEC (Forward Error Correction), which lets the
receiver detect and correct transmission errors on its own.

Each generation doubles bandwidth while tightening signal-integrity specs, which
demands higher-precision connector manufacturing. This creates a real barrier to
entry for high-speed connector suppliers.

High-speed cables emerging as a potential driver
ACES previously supplied connectors only. After several years of effort, its Gen6 PCIe
cable has now been qualified by two CSP clients, and the company began shipping the
in-rack cable product in April 2026. We expect high-speed cable shipments to enlarge
after the new capacity comes online and more CSP orders in 2H26. Hence, we expect
high-speed cable revenue to grow from NT$268m in 2026 to NT$793/NT$982m in

2027/28.
Currently, its high-speed cable product carries gross margin of merely 20%, but it is
likely to edge up to around 30% as the scale enlarges, and yield improves.

Capacity in place ahead of demand
ACES is rapidly expanding its high-speed cable and connector capacity, with high-
speed cable production lines expected to increase from two in 2025 to eight in
2H26, while it will proactively ramp up connector monthly capacity from 4m units
in July 2026 to 5m units by the end of 2026 to address customer demand ahead of
order placement.
Supported by strong customer demand and sufficient capacity, ACES expects total

connector shipments to reach 25m units in 2H26 and 50m units in 2027,
representing a significant increase from 15m units in 1H26.
As for power cables for Bloom Energy, ACES has leased a new facility next to its
Philippine plant, with the new power cable capacity planned to come online in
4Q26.

Diversified growth engines
ACES’s networking product portfolio includes set-top boxes, WiFi devices, routers,
and other networking equipment. We expect the business to benefit from
technology upgrades and equipment replacement driven by advances in wireless
communications, IoT, cloud computing, and cybersecurity. However, tariff and
inflationary pressures, together with component shortages among Asian customers,

are likely to limit growth. We expect networking product revenue to grow by 34%
and 14% in 2026 and 2027, respectively.

Automotive: rising electronic content
ACES’s product range spans ADAS, reversing radar and camera, body control, smart
cockpit, smart antenna, and high-voltage connectors and harnesses, extending into
applications such as EV charging guns. It has entered a US EV major as well as

European and Chinese Tier-1 supply chains. Going forward, it will continue to focus
on high-voltage, high-frequency, and high-speed connection technologies. The
company expects the automotive sector to stay flat in 2027 to reflect muted
demand and limited internal resource allocation, as it focuses on AI-related business
at this stage. In the long-term, growth from the automotive sector will be driven by
ADAS upgrades, which lifts demand for sensors and compute, alongside rising EV

penetration, which adds high-value electronic systems and power semiconductor
content.

Industrial: a steady UPS-driven base
ACES supplies industrial power cords, used mainly in uninterruptible power systems
(UPS). A UPS is an electrical device that acts as a reliable buffer when the main
power supply behaves abnormally, such as during a fault, outage, or unstable
voltage. It keeps automation controllers, sensors, and actuators continuously

powered so equipment keeps running. When a power problem occurs, the UPS
supplies stable backup power to prevent equipment failure, data corruption, or
system crashes.  We expect ACES’s industrial business to grow steadily, with growth
of 9% and 14% in 2026 and 2027, respectively.
Figure 29 Figure 30
ACES Industrial product portfolio Industrial sales and YoY
Source: ACES Source: SinoPac

cautiously optimistic about 2027 with the launch of Windows 12, combined with
easing component shortages and broader AI adoption, which should drive consumer
replacement demand, lifting global NB shipments -5%/5% year over year in
2027/28, which is a gradual recovery following a weaker 2026.

Power rack: leveraging its long-standing relationship with Schneider

ACES has a long-standing relationship with Schneider Electric at its Philippine plant.
The partnership dates back more than 20 years, with ACES’s Philippine plant
initially serving Schneider almost exclusively. Over the years, the product portfolio
has expanded from conventional power cables to uninterruptible power systems
(UPS) and, more recently, power racks for AI datacentres.
ACES began shipping high-voltage power cables for power racks in 1Q26 and

entered the volume ramp-up phase in 2Q26. We remain positive on power rack
adoption as the PSU in every IT tray hits a physical limit as rack power reaches
1MW, occupies rack space and caps compute density. Moving power shelves and
BBU/CBU into a dedicated power rack decouples power from computing and frees

Initiate with an Outperform rating
We initiate coverage of ACES with an Outperform rating given its dual growth
engine from Bloom’s strong expansion and high-speed connector customer wins
and generation upgrades. We forecast 2027/28 EPS of NT$11.19/NT$14.93, with
our 2027 forecast 48% ahead of consensus. Our TP of NT$270 is based on 20x
2H27-1H28SP PE, backed by a 72% EPS Cagr in 2026-28SP driven by Bloom’s

significant ramp and larger content in server products. Initiate coverage with an
Outperform rating.
Company profile
ACES was founded in 1996, and it mainly produces connectors, micro-coaxial cable,
high-speed transmission cables, and RF connectors. Connectors and cable
assemblies link two separate subsystems or operating units and carry the signals
between them, allowing the overall system to function properly. They are used
across a wide range of electronic products, with main applications spanning

notebooks and PC peripherals, consumer electronics, automotive electronics, cloud
servers, networking equipment, and industrial equipment.
To keep pace with the push towards lighter, thinner, and smaller notebooks and
consumer devices, ACES developed high-temperature-resistant, fine-pitch, and
low-profile products at an early stage. At the same time, it is moving actively into
the high-frequency, high-speed connectors, high-speed cables, and power supplies

required for cloud servers, as well as the connector and cable assemblies used in
automotive electronics.

Company development
ACES has used M&A well to gain new capabilities, plants and technology, which in
turn opened doors to new customers and markets. In 2015, it bought MEC IMEX
to add cable assembly capability and a plant in the Philippines, which took it into
the industrial market. In 2017, the CPC deal gave it access to the European auto
connector market and a plant in Vietnam. In 2019, it bought Kuang Ying to add

metal stamping capacity and reach Taiwan telecom customers. The key deal came
in 2021, when ACES bought 100% of Genesis, a US high-speed interconnect maker
based in Atlanta. The main goal was to gain Genesis’s long-standing ties with US
CSP and cloud customers. In 2025, ACES opened its Phase Two R&D headquarters
in Taoyuan, which focuses on cloud connectors and high-speed cables. We believe
10 years of M&A have turned ACES from a consumer connector maker into a

supplier of high-speed interconnect solutions for AI servers and the cloud.

Cashflow and balance sheet
ACES’s balance sheet shows healthy debt exposure with an improving net cash
level. Given its plan to massively expand capacity at its Taiwan plants, we expect
Capex to remain at a high level of NT$1,075m in 2026 and then slightly decline to
NT$855m in 2027. We forecast its depreciation-to-sales ratio to be around 3% in

2027, which is at the low end of its historical level.
We expect ACES’s ROE to expand from 10% in 2026 to 21% in 2027 and 23% in
2028, as the volume ramp of AI high-speed connectors and Bloom Energy power
cables drives a richer product mix and gross margin expansion, delivering 121% EPS
growth in 2027.
We expect ACES’s FCF to turn positive in 2026 as operating cashflow scales with
the AI high-speed connector and Bloom power cable ramp while Capex normalises

after the 2026 capacity buildout. Looking ahead, we expect ACES’s free cashflow
per share to be NT$0.8-1.5 in coming years.

Competitive positioning

In high-speed connectors, ACES competes primarily with overseas peers including
Amphenol (APH), TE, and Molex. APH’s edge lies in a development-technology lead of
one to two generations, while ACES’s relative strength rests on customers’ recognition
on delivering quality comparable to the overseas majors, alongside more flexible lead
times, strong capacity-expansion support, and fast ramp-up.
The structural opportunity comes from the supply chain integration trend. As

transmission speeds rise, CSPs increasingly prefer suppliers which can integrate high-
speed connectors with high-speed cables and ship assembled, finished goods.

### Page 29

Wired for growth ACES - O-PF
6 October 2026 29
ACES is one of the few Taiwanese players with this vertical-integration footprint: it
holds wire-drawing technology transferred in-house from ITRI, produces everything
from wire drawing through assembly on its own, and its commitment to moving into
high-speed cables has been noted by AWS and Meta, backed by roughly two years of
qualification work already completed.
By contrast, other Taiwanese peers have yet to build comparable vertical integration.

Customers have historically split connectors and cables across separate suppliers, with
cable suppliers represented by BizLink (3665 TT) and Longwell (6290 TT), while peers
such as Lotes (3533 TT), Alltop (3526 TT), Argosy (3217 TT) and JPC Connectivity
(6197 TT) are likewise strong in connectors but lack in-house cable capability.
Since the Gen5 qualified-supplier base was already fragmented, the Gen6 bar of
combined connector-plus-cable capability becomes ACES’s entry point, bringing ACES

into design-in from Gen6. The qualified connector supplier base is small, with suppliers
including APH, TE, and ACES, with Lotes added subsequently.
Peer comparison
ACES’s primary local competitors in connectors include Lotes, Alltop, Argosy and JPC
Connectivity. In the cable industry, it competes with Bizlink and Longwell. ACES’s
foreign peers include Amphenol, TE and FIT.

Target price calculation
We initiate coverage of ACES with an O-PF rating and a target price of NT$270,
based on 20x 2H27-1H28SP EPS, supported by a strong 72% EPS Cagr in 2026-

28SP. We use a PE-based methodology for ACES, in line with other connector
companies. We initiate coverage with an Outperform rating.
Our target multiple of 20x is in line with the 2027SP average PE of 20.4x for Taiwan-
listed connector and cable peers with direct AI datacentre exposure, namely
BizLink, Lotes and JPC Connectivity. We view this as a reasonable anchor. ACES
offers faster earnings growth than the group, with 2027SP EPS growth of 121%

versus the peer average of 52%, while its 2027-28SP ROE of 21-24% sits at the low
end of the peer range of 22-52%.

We also believe the target PE premium is justified as the AI revenue contribution,
including AI data transmission and power, rises from 4% in 2024 to 48% in 2027SP.
The company maintains its key supplier position at Bloom as the company ramps up
its capacity expansion, while ASIC customer wins and higher connector content
from the Gen6 upgrade cycle should provide additional growth drivers. As the
company gradually shifts away from its legacy consumer electronics base and its

exposure to cyclical downturns, we see room for a structural rerating.

Key risks
We see four key operational risks for ACES:
Raw material cost volatility: Copper prices drive a large share of Cogs for ACES's
cable products, while connectors carry far less exposure. ACES currently reprices
its cable products with customers on a quarterly basis, which has allowed it to

sustain a long-term GM of around 20% on power cables. However, the pass-through
might be lagging, so sharp copper price swings could cause quarterly GM to
fluctuate before repricing impact catches up.
Delays in ODM pull-in: ACES ships its high-speed connectors directly to ODM
customers. Because rack assembly sits at the final stage of the AI server supply
chain, any upstream disruption, such as components shortages and assembly yield
issue, could push out rack shipment schedules or reduce volumes. This could in turn

lead ODMs to cut or defer orders for high-speed connectors from ACES, creating
quarter-to-quarter volatility in server revenue.
Slower-than-expected ramp-up at Bloom Energy: Power cables for Bloom will
account for 28%/35% of ACES's revenue in 2027/28SP, which makes Bloom's
ramp-up the single largest swing factor in our forecasts. Delays could arise on both
the supply and demand sides. On the supply side, Bloom could fall behind its
capacity plan if new facility fit-out, equipment installation or hiring takes longer

than expected. On the demand side, its datacentre customers could defer
deployments due to grid interconnection, gas supply or financing hurdles, or due to
regulatory headwinds such as tighter permitting and emissions rules for on-site gas-
fired generation, utility restrictions on behind-the-meter power, or changes to fuel
cell tax incentives. Either scenario would directly reduce ACES's power cable
shipments. As volumes scale, Bloom may also qualify a second cable supplier, which

would add pressure to ACES's share and ASP.
Prolonged NB weakness: NB and Consumer remains ACES's largest segment at 35%
of 2026SP revenue, and our forecasts assume it returns to 4%/12% growth in
2027/28SP on a Windows 12-driven replacement cycle. If end-demand stays soft
or the replacement cycle slips, a further NB decline could offset part of the growth
from Bloom and AI servers and dilute group GM.

### Page 31

Wired for growth ACES - O-PF
6 October 2026 31
Investment thesis
ACES is evolving from a notebook connector maker into a supplier of AI
interconnects and power cables, with two growth engines ramping at the same
time. The first is Bloom Energy. ACES has co-developed SOFC power cables with
Bloom for about six years, and it is currently one of three suppliers of Bloom's power
cable assemblies, with around one-third of the total. It ships inner PCS and hot-box

cables in 2026 and adds external DLO high-voltage cables from 2027, with order
visibility extending to 2028 and beyond. The second engine is AI/ASIC servers. On
ASIC platforms, ODMs have more say in supplier selection, so ACES wins both
motherboard and daughterboard sockets, which lifts content from 10-20 to ~30
units per rack. Gen6 raises connector ASP by ~15%, and a new in-chassis PCIe Gen6
cable line, qualified by two CSPs, adds a higher-content stream. ACES integrates

vertically from wire drawing to final assembly, which makes it one of the few
Taiwanese suppliers able to ship connector-plus-cable solutions as CSPs
consolidate their vendor base.
Catalysts
(1) Bloom's capacity ramp from 2GW to 4GW/6GW in 2026-28 and new data-
center wins (Oracle, Nebius, AEP, Equinix) converting into cable orders; (2) DLO
high-voltage cable shipments starting in 2027; (3) ASIC customer ramps in 2H26
and a broader product range beyond MCIO into Multi-Track, board-to-board and

E1/E3 connectors; (4) rising Gen6 penetration on AI server trays; (5) high-speed
cable lines expanding from two to eight by end-2026, with GM improving from
~20% toward ~30%.
Valuation details
We adopt a PE-based valuation methodology as we believe the market will mainly
focus on the earnings growth outlooks of high-speed connector makers, which is
better reflected with PE metrics. We assign a target multiple of 20x PE, more than
+1sd of the three-year average, to reflect a strong outlook with margin expansion

and an EPS Cagr of 72% in 2026-28 on solid Bloom Energy demand and increasing
high speed connector content.
Investment risks
There could be downside risk to sales growth and margins if: (1) Bloom's
deployments or backlog conversion slow on financing, gas supply or permitting
delays, or ACES loses share among Bloom's cable suppliers; (2) ASIC programs ramp
more slowly or CSP Capex is cut; (3) Gen6 adoption is slower than expected, or
competition from Amphenol, TE, Molex and Lotes pressures ASP; (4) high-speed

cable yield and GM improve more slowly than we assume; (5) NB and consumer
demand weakens further on component shortages, tariffs and inflation; (6) TWD
appreciation or higher copper prices squeeze margins.

#consumer #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ household incomes, assets and savings
update - June quarter 2026
By Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
10:24 October 08 2026 Share
New Zealand incomes and savings are rising, as is the value of households’ financial
assets. However, the value of housing assets remains flat.
Key takeouts from Stats NZ’s latest update on household incomes, assets and savings (June quarter
2026)

### Page 2

Household incomes
Disposable income levels in the economy grew by 5.2% in the year to June, down from 6.5% in the
year to March

Savings and wealth
Household savings have continued to rise, climbing by $2.5b over the past quarter.

Overall household wealth levels were up 1.9% over the past year. Under the surface, the make-up of
household wealth continues to shift.

Interest costs 
Overall household income levels are continuing to rise. Much of that is due to increases in
entrepreneurial earnings (which is in part due to increased earnings in the agricultural sector). Wage
and salary growth has been more limited but has picked up.
Household savings levels have been rising for three years now, with another solid rise over the past
three months. 
Overall household wealth levels have risen modestly. Although the value of households’ financial
assets is continuing to rise, housing assets have essentially shown no growth since late 2023.
Household spending on interest costs rose just slightly in the June quarter. That’s the first increase in
two years. The easing in average borrowing costs seen over the past few years has come to a close
with the RBNZ’s hiking cycle now in train.
Underpinning the rise in overall income levels have been continued increases in entrepreneurial
earnings, which are up 12% over the past year. That will include stronger earnings in the agricultural
sector, which has been boosted by the strength in commodity export prices. That lift in agricultural
earnings has been reflected in stronger economic confidence and spending in many rural regions.
We expect commodity export earnings will remain firm over the year ahead. 
In contrast, wage and salary earnings are up just 2.7% over the past year. That’s up from 1.8% in the
year to March, but it’s a fairly muted rise, especially given the continued increases in the population
and inflation of 4.1%.
June’s large increase in savings builds on the solid increases seen over the past three years.
The savings rate in the June quarter was 3.7% of disposable incomes, down just slightly from last
quarter.
The value of households’ financial assets has continued to rise and is up 4.6% over the past year. 
However, the value of housing and land assets is down 0.9% over the past year, and it has remained
essentially unchanged since late 2023. We expect housing market conditions will remain soft over
the coming year, limiting increases in overall household wealth. 
Households’ spending on interest costs rose just slightly in the June quarter, climbing by 0.3%.
While that’s a relatively small change, it is the first increase that we’ve seen in two years.

### Page 3

Related articles

Media Contact:
Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist
P: +64 9 336 5668
M: +64 21 710 852
Over the past few years, many borrowers saw their debt servicing costs fall as they rolled off earlier
high mortgage rates and onto lower ones. That helped to support household spending.
However, the easing in average borrowing cost has now ended. Recent months have seen mortgage
rates pushing higher as the RBNZ has begun to hike the Official Cash Rate, with further hikes
expected over the coming year. 
Looking ahead, we don’t expect a significant increase in average household borrowing costs.
However, we won’t see the same support for spending that we did over the past few years.

## Document 296: Persistent sources of inflation and the RBNZ (2026-10-13)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Persistent sources of inflation and the RBNZ (2026-10-13).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-13
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1
Westpac New Zealand Economics
A lack of headroom: The RBNZ, oil prices and administered costs.
• New Zealand households have faced a run of
large cost increases in recent years related
to administered charges and parts of the
economy that are less exposed to competition,
such as electricity charges and local
council rates.
• Those cost increases have continued for an
extended period, making them hard for the
RBNZ to look through.
• This has also meant that the RBNZ had little

headroom to absorb upside inflation surprises
even before the recent spike in oil prices.
• We expect that there will be ongoing sizeable
increases in many of these costs. That is a
key reason why we expect domestic (non-
tradables) inflation will be higher than the
RBNZ expects over the longer-term.
• That ongoing firmness in domestic inflation
is also a reason why we expect the RBNZ
will ultimately need to raise the OCR by
more than they assumed in their September

policy Statement.
• But getting inflation down to target levels
in the face of an oil price spike and high
administered prices won’t be easy and involves
some tough trade-offs. Administered prices
tend to be less responsive to changes in
economic conditions. As a result, interest
rate hikes are likely to have a bigger impact
on parts of the economy that are not the main
drivers of inflation.
Inflation is high for reasons beyond
oil prices.
Inflation in New Zealand rose to 4.1% in the year to June,

the highest it’s been in three years. And it’s set to remain
above 3% until at least mid-2027.
Much of the recent strength in inflation has been due
to the spike in oil prices since the outbreak of war in the
Middle East. However, even before the spike in fuel costs,
inflation had been running hot for an extended period:
inflation has been above 3% since mid-2025, and it’s now
been above the RBNZ’s 2% target midpoint for five years.
That persistent firmness in inflation is particularly notable

as, based on RBNZ estimates, the New Zealand economy
has had a negative output gap since September 2024,
with softness in economic growth and a weak labour
market. Those conditions would typically be associated
with more moderate inflation than we’ve seen.

Looking beneath the surface, much of the strength
in inflation over the past year has been related to
administered costs, such as government charges, and
price increases in parts of the economy that are less
1 Oct 2026 | Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5668 | +64 21 710 852 | satish.ranchhod@westpac.co.nz
ECONOMIC BULLETIN
Persistent inflation and the RBNZ.

### Page 2

2
Westpac New Zealand Economics
exposed to competitive pressures. For instance, over the
past year, local council rates rose nearly 9%, electricity
prices rose by 12% and health insurance premiums rose
by 19%.
The less competitive nature of some of these sectors
means they aren’t very responsive to changes in interest
rates. Instead, they are most likely to behave in a “cost-
plus” manner. And in some cases, recent years have seen
large increases in operating costs that are now being

passed on to consumers.
These aren’t one-off cost rises the RBNZ can
look through.
Just as the RBNZ might look through a one-off spike
in oil prices, there’s a reasonable argument for looking
through a one-off rise in administered prices that pushes
up inflation. For instance, a one-off increase in vehicle
registration costs might boost inflation temporarily, but
isn’t something the RBNZ would respond to.
However, the issue here is that the increases we’re seeing

are not one-off price rises. We’ve seen large increases
spread across a range of administered costs for several
years now (see chart on page 4). That’s been reflected
in measures of core inflation, which strip out swings in
volatile items like fuel and instead track the underlying
trend in prices. For example, excluding the rise in food
and fuel prices, inflation in the year to June was 2.9%,
and it’s been running close to or above 3% for five years

now. That’s meant that the RBNZ had little headroom to
absorb upside inflation surprises even before the recent
spike in fuel costs.
Headline and core

We expect that there will be continued large increases
in some administered costs. For instance, in the case
of local council rates, many councils will need to fund
significant infrastructure spending over the coming

years. There have been efforts to limit the extent of those
increases, such as the Government’s cap on council
rates. However, that doesn’t change the underlying
reason for the large rates increases in recent years, such
as increased spending on essential infrastructure. And if
councils can’t raise the required revenue through rates,
they may have to look at other approaches like user
pays. That could shift costs for households, rather than
reducing them.

Why does this matter?
It’s important to remember that the RBNZ’s focus is not

individual prices in the CPI (like fuel prices or council
rates), but the overall level of inflation. In essence, it
doesn’t matter why inflation is elevated – all that matters
is that it is elevated and how persistent inflation will be.
Right now, with core inflation already elevated, it’s hard
for the RBNZ to look through the large and continuing
cost increases that we’ve seen across a range of areas
that are less exposed to competition. And given the risk

of ongoing large increases in these costs, we expect that
domestic (non-tradables) inflation will be higher than the
RBNZ has assumed over the longer-term.
That’s part of the reason why we continue to expect that
the RBNZ will need to raise the Official Cash Rate by
more than they had assumed in their September policy
Statement. We’re forecasting a 25bp rate rise at the
RBNZ’s December meeting and expect the OCR will peak
at 4% next year. That risk is being compounded by the

protracted nature of the current rise in oil prices, along
with other risks such as El Niño which could boost food
prices over the coming year.
However, costs like oil prices, electricity charges, and
administered charges like council rates are not responsive
to changes in interest rates. As a result, when inflation
pressures are centred on such areas, the RBNZ needs
to work harder to keep overall inflation in check. It does
this by leaning against inflation in areas of the economy

that are more responsive to interest rates to bring
aggregate inflation pressures down towards target in a
timely manner. But rather than leaning against the source
of inflation pressures, the impact of tighter policy will
be to dampen demand and price-setting in other more

### Page 3

3
Westpac New Zealand Economics
interest rate-sensitive parts of the economy such as the
construction sector or discretionary retail spending.
This is an uncomfortable but necessary trade-off. Tighter
policy will weigh on discretionary spending, the labour
market and near-term growth, and would affect prices in
parts of the economy that are not the drivers of inflation.
A higher OCR also means increases in households’ debt-
servicing costs, and for some households, that will be

very challenging.
However, looking through or not responding to these
sorts of ongoing large cost increases would still leave
households dealing with significant increases in overall
living costs. In the long run, that could be more damaging
for households and the economy.

Forecasts
Adding to the RBNZ’s concerns about both the oil price
shock and continued large increases in administered
prices, inflation expectations are relatively elevated. In
fact, long-term inflation expectations in business and
consumer surveys are close to where they were in 2022
when inflation was much higher than today. Similarly,
firms consistently signal in business surveys that cost
pressures are strong and margins have been squeezed.
Business pricing intentions vs consumer prices

Softness in demand might be limiting some firms’ ability
to lift prices at the current time. However, when demand
eventually firms again, there’s a risk that businesses
expect continued high levels of inflation and try to
push through larger price rises (sometimes referred to

as high inflation expectations becoming ‘embedded’).
That means inflation could remain high even when the
immediate impact of temporary boosts to inflation, like
an oil price spike, fades. This is a key reason why it’s
important to get inflation back to 2% on a consistent
basis, rather than allowing inflation to run at levels closer
to the top of the RBNZ’s target band.
The RBNZ does have discretion in how aggressively it
responds to inflation, and that is where the source of

inflation matters. The RBNZ is likely to adjust the OCR
more gradually when inflation is related to less interest
rate-sensitive areas, like oil costs or government charges.
And when doing so, it does account for how changes in
the Official Cash Rate could affect the labour market and
economic activity. But even if the RBNZ responds more
gradually to inflation, it can’t ignore ongoing large cost
increases indefinitely. Eventually inflation needs to be

brought back to target.
See overleaf for a chart of how consumer prices have
changed over the past 25 years.

Source PDF: all report pdf/Sales Trader Commentary_ Riding the TAIEX 50k Tailwind _ GSTW.pdf

### Page 1

Sales Trader Commentary: Riding the TAIEX 50k
Tailwind | GSTW
6 October 2026 | 1:15 PM Taiwan Standard Time
Robert Wang
Goldman Sachs (Asia) L.L.C. Taipei Branch
FICC & Equities
TAIEX futures officially touched 50k points for the first time, and the index is only
inches away. Local sentiment feels celebratory with higher level of participation, and
multiple catalysts coming up to edge the index higher. Global markets are reacting
well to the macro data (i.e. NFP on Friday). Here's what I am watching for the next two

weeks and some investor feedback:
Key Investor Feedback and Observation:
Major Event Coming Up. TSMC will report monthly revenue on Thursday and hold
earnings call on 10/15. We have raised our TP with a earnings preview note. We are
forecasting sequential growth into 4Q26 with slight GM dilution from N2 ramp. See
our full note here.
Flows and Positioning. Foreigners net bought US$2.3bn yesterday, the most in the
past 2 weeks and some clear rotation into large caps. TSMC returned as top bought

of close to US$800mn vs last week net outflows of US$500mn. From a monthly
perspective, Taiwan saw modest net buying alongside strong risk-on activity,
with hedge funds adding both long and short positions. Net allocation ended
the month at 7.2% (79%ile 1Y, 96%ile 5Y), while gross allocation finished at
4.5% (93%ile 1Y, 99%ile 5Y). See below exhibit for more details. Retail margin
balance kept refreshing record high until 10/5.
Secular Growth Sectors Catching Up. MLCC and Wafers broke out recently. Same

narrative - shortage, price hike, etc. When the expectation is low enough, it is easy
to trigger some quick inflows and technical rebound due to short covering. From
the fundamental perspective, components (i.e. Memory, MLCC, ABF, CPU) still have
supply constraint per Trendforce data. We can expect these companies to deliver
good top line and bottom line.
Some Long Ideas from the Desk.
Long TSMC Capex names - cleanroom to semi equipment should see some +ve

price action in the coming week. Top idea All Ring (6187 TT) on the advanced
packaging need with good order visibility from both TSMC and ASE.
Long Power - Delta (2308 TT) and Lite-On (2301 TT) should see secular
growth demand once 800/400VDC is deployed. Don't forget about fuel cells as
alternative energy sources - Buy Kaori (8996 TT)
Value Buy - long the underperformed hardware names such as Accton (2345
TT), Lotes (3533 TT), and GCE (2368 TT). Much better risk reward level at

below 20x Forward P/E valuations.
Watch Out for Volatility in High Price Stocks. We are seeing some profit taking in
recent outperformers such as Mediatek and GUC. Aggressive investors may even

Risk Management. A lot of investors are still worried about high treasury yield and
climbing oil price. Indeed, global market breadth is narrow; only AI complex trades
are outperforming. Fortunately, Taiwan is the top beneficiary of this whole AI
growth. Note that we have couple long weekends coming up in Taiwan, so investors
may take some chips off the table. Nonetheless, my view remains unchanged -
bright future ahead with upcoming earnings to back the momentum.

TPCA Show 2026. Taiwan annual PCB show is coming up on 10/20 - 22. Many
PCB/ABF players will participate at the show, while brokers will host events and
analyst meetings. Expect +VE catalyst in this sub sector.

performance) of their underlying index or benchmark during the same period of time. This effect can be magnified in volatile markets.

condition persists in the market for a commodity that creates a disparity between near-term future prices and long-term future prices
and may lead to unexpected performance results. Other factors, such as roll yield, transaction costs, management fees, and taxes may
cause deviation in performance between the spot price of a commodity and commodity futures. You should not assume that an ETF or
ETN that is linked to commodity futures will provide an effective hedge because of a negative correlation with equities or other asset

swaps, and other derivatives as well as non-investment-grade securities - give rise to substantial risk and are not available to nor suitable

The scenario, forecasts and nowcasts of the Economic Research
